## Practices

- [ABCDE Method](https://www.briantracy.com/blog/time-management/the-abcde-list-technique-for-setting-priorities/) - Task prioritization technique by Brian Tracy that categorizes tasks into 5 categories (A through E) based on importance and consequences. Featured in 'Eat That Frog!' as a core productivity principle. ([Read more](/details/abcde-method.md)) `Prioritization` `Productivity` `Task Management` `Decision Making`
- [Cal Newport Time Blocking](https://www.timeblockplanner.com/) - Deep work time management methodology by Cal Newport involving dividing each day into time blocks with specific tasks assigned to each block. Creates structured schedules that generate massive productivity gains. ([Read more](/details/cal-newport-time-blocking.md)) `Time Blocking` `Deep Work` `productivity-method`
- [Context Switching Penalty](https://www.apa.org/news/press/releases/2006/03/multitasking) - Cognitive cost incurred when switching between different tasks or projects, including attention residue, ramp-up time, and reduced performance. Research shows switching can cost 20-40% of productive time. ([Read more](/details/context-switching-penalty.md)) `cognitive-science` `Productivity` `context-switching`
- [Covey Time Management Matrix](https://www.franklincovey.com/) - Prioritization framework created by Stephen Covey that divides tasks into four quadrants based on urgency and importance. Featured in 'The 7 Habits of Highly Effective People,' it helps identify top-priority tasks for optimized productivity. ([Read more](/details/covey-time-management-matrix.md)) `methodology` `Prioritization` `Decision Making`
- [Deep Work & Shallow Work Separation](https://calnewport.com/deep-work-rules-for-focused-success-in-a-distracted-world) - Productivity framework by Cal Newport that distinguishes between cognitively demanding deep work and low-value shallow work, advocating for dedicated time blocks and minimization of the latter. ([Read more](/details/deep-work-shallow-work-separation.md)) `Deep Work` `Focus` `Productivity`
- [Deep Work Methodology](https://calnewport.com/) - Productivity philosophy by Cal Newport emphasizing sustained, distraction-free concentration on cognitively demanding tasks. Argues that deep work is becoming rare yet increasingly valuable in the modern economy. ([Read more](/details/deep-work-methodology.md)) `Deep Work` `Focus` `cognitive-efficiency`
- [Don't Break the Chain (Seinfeld Strategy)](https://hubstaff.com/blog/dont-break-the-chain/) - Habit-building method attributed to Jerry Seinfeld using visual tracking on a calendar. Mark each completed day with an X to create a chain; motivation comes from not breaking the growing streak. ([Read more](/details/dont-break-the-chain.md)) `habits` `consistency` `Visualization` `motivation`
- [Eat That Frog Method](https://www.briantracy.com/blog/time-management/the-truth-about-frogs/) - Productivity methodology by Brian Tracy focused on tackling your biggest, most important task first thing each morning. The 'frog' is the task you're most likely to procrastinate on, and eating it eliminates the day's biggest challenge early. ([Read more](/details/eat-that-frog-method.md)) `methodology` `Prioritization` `Procrastination`
- [Focus Time Method](https://www.getclockwise.com/blog/focus-time) - Productivity practice of blocking dedicated time for deep, uninterrupted work on important tasks. Defends calendar blocks from meetings and distractions to enable sustained concentration on cognitively demanding work. ([Read more](/details/focus-time-method.md)) `focus-time` `Deep Work` `calendar-blocking`
- [Getting Things Done (GTD)](https://gettingthingsdone.com/) - Comprehensive time management system by David Allen consisting of five steps: capture, clarify, organize, reflect, and engage. An all-in-one system of lists and calendars for both work and personal life. ([Read more](/details/getting-things-done.md)) `Productivity` `methodology` `Task Management` `organization`
- [Ivy Lee Method](https://jamesclear.com/ivy-lee) - Century-old productivity technique involving writing down six most important tasks each evening and tackling them in order the next day. Created in the early 1900s for Charles Schwab, it's the foundation of modern time blocking. ([Read more](/details/ivy-lee-method.md)) `methodology` `Prioritization` `Simplicity`
- [Maker's Schedule vs Manager's Schedule](http://www.paulgraham.com/makersschedule.html) - Framework by Paul Graham distinguishing between maker schedule (long uninterrupted blocks for creative work) and manager schedule (day divided into hourly intervals). Explains why meetings are particularly disruptive for makers. ([Read more](/details/makers-schedule-vs-managers-schedule.md)) `methodology` `Focus` `meeting-management`
- [MIT Method (Most Important Tasks)](https://zenhabits.net/purpose-your-day-most-important-task/) - Time management strategy popularized by Leo Babauta focusing on identifying 1-3 most important tasks each day. Emphasizes doing MITs first thing in the morning during peak cognitive hours for maximum impact. ([Read more](/details/mit-method.md)) `Prioritization` `Productivity` `morning` `impact`
- [Pareto Principle (80/20 Rule)](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-life-hacks/organization/understanding-pareto-principle-of-time-management) - Concept by Vilfredo Pareto stating that 80% of outcomes result from 20% of causes. In time management, 20% of efforts produce 80% of results, guiding focus on high-impact activities. ([Read more](/details/pareto-principle.md)) `Productivity` `Prioritization` `Efficiency` `Focus`
- [Parkinson's Law](https://asana.com/resources/parkinsons-law) - Observation by Cyril Northcote Parkinson (1955) that work expands to fill the time available for its completion. Applied to time management through strategic deadline setting and timeboxing. ([Read more](/details/parkinsons-law.md)) `Productivity` `Time Management` `Deadlines` `Efficiency`
- [Time Management Matrix](https://www.franklincovey.com/the-7-habits/habit-3/) - Framework for categorizing tasks based on urgency and importance, popularized by Stephen Covey. Also known as the Eisenhower Matrix or Urgent-Important Matrix, this tool helps prioritize work by distinguishing between what requires immediate attention and what drives long-term success. ([Read more](/details/time-management-matrix.md)) `Prioritization` `frameworks` `Decision Making`
- [Timeboxing Method](https://trackingtime.co/time-tracking-software/time-boxing.html) - Flexible time management technique rooted in agile principles that involves allocating specific, limited time to tasks with no possibility of exceeding the limit. Improves focus, reduces overthinking, and encourages faster decision-making. ([Read more](/details/timeboxing-method.md)) `methodology` `timeboxing` `Agile`
- [Ultradian Rhythm 90-Minute Cycles](https://www.asianefficiency.com/productivity/ultradian-rhythms/) - Science-based productivity method using natural 90-minute biological cycles for focused work sessions. Based on ultradian rhythms, it optimizes cognitive performance through work-rest cycles aligned with body's natural patterns. ([Read more](/details/ultradian-rhythm-90-minute-cycles.md)) `science-based` `biology` `Focus`
- [Warren Buffett's 5/25 Rule](https://friday.app/p/5-25-rule) - Prioritization exercise attributed to Warren Buffett where you list 25 goals, circle your top 5, and actively avoid the remaining 20. Emphasizes extreme focus by eliminating good opportunities to concentrate on the best ones. ([Read more](/details/warren-buffett-525-rule.md)) `Prioritization` `Focus` `goals` `elimination`
- [1-3-5 Rule](https://hubstaff.com/blog/1-3-5-rule/) - A productivity technique that involves completing one major task, three medium tasks, and five minor tasks each day. This task management method emphasizes prioritization and focus by limiting daily tasks to nine well-defined items that align with your brain's natural capacity. ([Read more](/details/1-3-5-rule.md)) `productivity-method` `Task Management` `Prioritization`
- [1-3-9 Method](https://www.frontlinesourcegroup.com/blog-what-is-the-best-way-to-prioritize-work.html) - A powerful task prioritization framework that limits daily focus to 13 manageable tasks: one critical priority, three important tasks, and nine smaller tasks to ensure proper attention allocation across different priority levels. ([Read more](/details/1-3-9-method.md)) `Prioritization` `Focus` `daily-planning`
- [10-10-10 Rule](https://www.inc.com/marcel-schwantes/how-emotionally-intelligent-leaders-use-the-10-10-10-rule-to-make-the-right-decisions/91070239) - Decision-making framework by Suzy Welch that evaluates choices by considering their impact in 10 minutes, 10 months, and 10 years. This method enables logical, grounded decisions by balancing short-term demands with long-term vision, eradicating rash decision-making. ([Read more](/details/10-10-10-rule.md)) `Decision Making` `Time Management` `strategic-thinking`
- [12 Week Year Method](https://12weekyear.com/) - A productivity and goal-setting system developed by Brian P. Moran and Michael Lennington that redefines your year to be 12 weeks long, eliminating procrastination through increased urgency and shortened planning cycles to achieve more in less time. ([Read more](/details/12-week-year-method.md)) `goal-setting` `Planning` `productivity-method`
- [168 Hours Method](https://lauravanderkam.com/books/168-hours/) - Time management technique that reframes weekly planning by treating each week as 168 total hours, helping users budget time intentionally across work, sleep, and personal priorities. ([Read more](/details/168-hours-method.md)) `methodology` `weekly-planning` `time-budgeting`
- [18-Minute Plan](https://bregmanpartners.com/18minutes/) - The 18-Minute Plan is a daily productivity ritual created by Peter Bregman consisting of 5 minutes of morning planning, 1 minute of refocus every hour for 8 hours, and 5 minutes of evening review to manage your day and master distraction. ([Read more](/details/18-minute-plan.md)) `daily-planning` `Focus` `reflection` `Time Management`
- [2-List Strategy (Buffett)](https://jamesclear.com/buffett-focus) - Warren Buffett's prioritization method where you list 25 goals, circle the top 5 as your focus, and treat the remaining 20 as items to avoid at all costs until the top 5 are achieved. ([Read more](/details/2-list-strategy-buffett.md)) `Prioritization` `Focus` `elimination`
- [2x/3x Rule](https://theknowledge.io/project-planning-with-hofstadters-law-and-the-2x-3x-rule/) - A time estimation guideline suggesting you should double or triple your initial estimate for complex projects to account for unforeseen delays, interruptions, and complications, directly addressing the planning fallacy and Hofstadter's Law. ([Read more](/details/2x3x-rule.md)) `estimation` `Planning` `Project Management` `buffer-time`
- [3-2-1 Rule (Mel Robbins)](https://www.melrobbins.com/podcasts/episode-38) - Evening routine productivity technique by Mel Robbins for better sleep and next-day performance: stop eating/drinking alcohol 3 hours before bed, stop working 2 hours before bed, shut down screens 1 hour before bed. ([Read more](/details/3-2-1-rule-mel-robbins.md)) `evening-routine` `sleep-optimization` `Productivity`
- [3-3-3 Method](https://www.inc.com/jessica-stillman/productivity-time-management-oliver-burkeman-333-method.html) - Productivity framework that structures your workday into three hours of deep work, three shorter tasks, and three maintenance activities. Popularized by Oliver Burkeman to align effort with natural energy levels. ([Read more](/details/3-3-3-method.md)) `productivity-method` `Deep Work` `timeboxing`
- [4D System](https://www.insightful.io/blog/4d-system-time-tracking) - A time management technique that prioritizes tasks with four actions: Delete, Delegate, Defer, and Do. This system helps filter and manage workload more effectively by categorizing every task into one of these four action categories. ([Read more](/details/4d-system.md)) `Prioritization` `Decision Making` `Workflow`
- [5-Second Rule](https://www.melrobbins.com/the-5-second-rule/) - Anti-procrastination technique created by Mel Robbins that uses a simple countdown mechanism to overcome hesitation and initiate action. The method involves counting backwards from 5 to 1, then immediately taking physical action before the brain can create excuses or self-doubt. ([Read more](/details/5-second-rule.md)) `Procrastination` `methodology` `psychology`
- [52-17 Rule](https://desktime.com/blog/52-17-updated) - Work-break productivity ratio discovered by DeskTime in 2014 research. Most productive employees work for 52 minutes, then break for 17 minutes. The ratio has evolved to 75/33 in recent studies. ([Read more](/details/52-17-rule.md)) `Productivity` `Breaks` `research-based` `Focus`
- [52/17 Rule](https://desktime.com/blog/52-17-updated) - A productivity methodology suggesting working in focused 52-minute blocks followed by 17-minute breaks, based on DeskTime's 2014 study of top performers' work patterns. ([Read more](/details/5217-rule.md)) `Productivity` `Breaks` `Focus`
- [5AM Club Method](https://www.robinsharma.com/book/the-5am-club) - Morning routine framework by Robin Sharma featuring the 20-20-20 formula: 20 minutes exercise, 20 minutes reflection, 20 minutes learning. Emphasizes early rising for peak productivity. ([Read more](/details/5am-club-method.md)) `morning-routine` `early-rising` `personal-development` `habits`
- [6-Minute Billing Increment](https://www.clio.com/blog/billable-hour) - Professional-services standard billing method where one hour equals ten 0.1-hour segments, commonly used by lawyers, accountants, and consultants for precise billable time tracking. ([Read more](/details/6-minute-billing-increment.md)) `billing-practice` `legal` `Professional Services`
- [60/40 Rule (Brendon Burchard)](https://brendon.com/blog/60-40-rule-time-management/) - Time management principle by Brendon Burchard stating that high performers spend 60% of their time on needle-moving activities that drive progress and 40% on regular tasks, preventing burnout while maintaining effectiveness. ([Read more](/details/6040-rule-brendon-burchard.md)) `Time Management` `high-performance` `Prioritization`
- [70/30 Rule](https://compasscoachingllc.com/the-70-30-principle/) - A strengths-based time management principle where individuals allocate 70% of their time to tasks that align with natural strengths and energize them, while 30% is spent on growth-oriented challenges. Also applies to work-life balance with 70% quality time for primary focus and 30% for wellbeing and flexibility. ([Read more](/details/70-30-rule.md)) `Energy Management` `strengths-based` `Work Life Balance`
- [80/20 Rule (Pareto Principle)](https://www.investopedia.com/terms/1/80-20-rule.asp) - Principle stating that 80% of results come from 20% of efforts. Applied to time management by identifying and focusing on the vital few activities that produce most of your desired outcomes. ([Read more](/details/8020-rule-pareto-principle.md)) `Prioritization` `Efficiency` `effectiveness`
- [90-90-1 Rule](https://www.workzone.com/blog/90-90-1-rule/) - Productivity technique developed by Robin Sharma that focuses on dedicating the first 90 minutes of your work day to your single most important opportunity for the next 90 days. Aligns with ultradian rhythms and peak productivity hours. ([Read more](/details/90-90-1-rule.md)) `productivity-method` `Focus` `morning-routine`
- [90-Day Planning](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/time-management-strategies) - Strategic planning methodology using 90-day cycles to set goals, track progress, and maintain focus. Shorter than annual planning but long enough for meaningful progress, this approach improves execution and adaptability. ([Read more](/details/90-day-planning.md)) `Planning` `goal-setting` `quarterly`
- [90-Day Planning Cycles](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/time-management-strategies) - Quarterly goal-setting and execution framework that balances long-term vision with short-term achievement, providing regular review points and course correction opportunities. ([Read more](/details/90-day-planning-cycles.md)) `quarterly` `Planning` `goals`
- [90-Minute Focus Sessions](https://www.asianefficiency.com/productivity/ultradian-rhythms/) - A productivity methodology based on ultradian rhythms, the natural 90-120 minute cycles during which the brain alternates between high alertness and recovery periods. ([Read more](/details/90-minute-focus-sessions.md)) `Focus` `Productivity` `Neuroscience`
- [Accountability Partner System](https://jamesclear.com/accountability-partner) - Productivity practice involving regular check-ins with a partner or group to report progress, share goals, and maintain commitment to objectives. Leverages social accountability to increase follow-through on time management and productivity goals. ([Read more](/details/accountability-partner-system.md)) `accountability` `habits` `goal-achievement`
- [Action Method](https://www.larksuite.com/en_us/topics/productivity-glossary/action-method) - Productivity system created by Behance founder Scott Belsky that organizes projects into Action Steps, Backburner Items, and References. Emphasizes bias toward action by breaking everything into concrete next steps, ensuring projects move forward rather than remaining aspirational. ([Read more](/details/action-method.md)) `project-organization` `Task Management` `methodology`
- [Action Priority Matrix](https://www.mindtools.com/ajz128x/the-action-priority-matrix) - Decision-making tool that plots tasks on a matrix based on effort required and impact delivered. Helps identify quick wins, major projects, fill-ins, and tasks to eliminate for optimal resource allocation. ([Read more](/details/action-priority-matrix.md)) `Prioritization` `Decision Making` `resource-allocation`
- [Activity-Based Costing with Time Tracking](https://www.investopedia.com/terms/a/abc.asp) - Financial management method using detailed time tracking data to allocate costs to specific activities and projects, providing accurate profitability analysis and pricing insights. ([Read more](/details/activity-based-costing-with-time-tracking.md)) `cost-accounting` `profitability` `pricing`
- [Agile Results](https://gettingresults.com/) - Outcomes-focused productivity framework by JD Meier developed over 20+ years at Microsoft, centered on The Rule of 3 and weekly rhythms to help you achieve meaningful results with better focus, energy, and work-life balance. ([Read more](/details/agile-results.md)) `productivity-method` `outcomes` `Agile` `Focus`
- [Agile Story Points & Velocity](https://www.atlassian.com/agile/project-management/estimation) - A relative estimation method in Agile that measures complexity, effort, and risk rather than time, using techniques like Planning Poker and tracking team velocity for predictable sprint planning. ([Read more](/details/agile-story-points-velocity.md)) `Agile` `estimation` `scrum`
- [AI Scheduling](https://reclaim.ai/blog/ai-scheduling) - Methodology using artificial intelligence to automatically schedule tasks and meetings based on priorities, deadlines, and calendar availability. AI scheduling optimizes time allocation and reduces scheduling conflicts. ([Read more](/details/ai-scheduling.md)) `Ai Powered` `Automation` `methodology`
- [ALPEN Method](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/aplen-time-management-technique) - A simple but powerful time management technique developed by Lothar J. Seiwert that uses five steps to create effective daily plans in just a few minutes per day. ([Read more](/details/alpen-method.md)) `Time Management` `Planning` `Productivity`
- [Analog Time Tracking Methods](https://bulletjournal.com/) - Paper-based and physical time tracking approaches using notebooks, planners, time logs, and manual timesheets. Analog methods offer tangible, distraction-free tracking without digital tools, preferred by those seeking simplicity or reduced screen time. ([Read more](/details/analog-time-tracking-methods.md)) `analog` `manual` `Paper Based`
- [Analogous Estimation](https://www.atlassian.com/work-management/project-management/project-estimation) - Project planning technique using historical data from similar past projects to estimate duration and resource needs, leveraging organizational knowledge for more accurate time predictions. ([Read more](/details/analogous-estimation.md)) `estimation` `historical-data` `Planning`
- [Annual Hours Contracts](https://www.acas.org.uk/) - Employment arrangement where workers commit to a total number of hours per year rather than fixed weekly schedules. Provides flexibility for both employers and employees to accommodate seasonal demand fluctuations and personal preferences while ensuring annual workload commitments. ([Read more](/details/annual-hours-contracts.md)) `annual-hours` `flexible-contracts` `Workforce Management`
- [Async Time Tracking](https://async.twist.com/asynchronous-communication/) - Time tracking methodology designed for asynchronous remote teams where work happens across time zones and flexible schedules. Focuses on outcomes and self-reported time rather than real-time monitoring. ([Read more](/details/async-time-tracking.md)) `Remote Work` `async` `distributed-teams`
- [Async Work Time Tracking](https://async.twist.com/) - Time tracking approach for asynchronous workflows where team members work across time zones and schedules without real-time coordination. Emphasizes outcome tracking over hours, flexible schedules, and documentation-first communication while maintaining accountability. ([Read more](/details/async-work-time-tracking.md)) `async` `Remote Work` `flexible`
- [Async-First Communication](https://async.twist.com/asynchronous-communication/) - Work philosophy prioritizing asynchronous communication methods over real-time synchronous interactions. Enables flexible schedules, reduces meeting burden, and protects focus time while maintaining team collaboration and productivity. ([Read more](/details/async-first-communication.md)) `Remote Work` `communication` `Productivity`
- [Asynchronous Time Tracking](https://async.twist.com/asynchronous-communication/) - Time management approach for distributed teams working across time zones, enabling flexible scheduling and async collaboration while maintaining accountability and productivity metrics. ([Read more](/details/asynchronous-time-tracking.md)) `async` `distributed-teams` `Remote Work`
- [Asynchronous Work Methodology](https://async.twist.com/asynchronous-communication/) - Modern work practice that enables teams to collaborate effectively without requiring simultaneous online presence. Async work methodology prioritizes written documentation, flexible schedules, and thoughtful communication over real-time meetings and instant responses, resulting in more productive deep work time and better work-life balance for distributed teams. ([Read more](/details/asynchronous-work-methodology.md)) `Remote Work` `communication` `Productivity`
- [Atomic Habits Time Tracking](https://jamesclear.com/atomic-habits) - Habit-building approach based on James Clear's Atomic Habits principles applied to time tracking, using habit stacking, identity-based goals, and the 1% improvement rule. ([Read more](/details/atomic-habits-time-tracking.md)) `habits` `Productivity` `methodology`
- [Attention Management Method](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/time-management-strategies) - Modern productivity framework focusing on managing attention and focus rather than just time, recognizing that quality of attention determines output quality in knowledge work environments. ([Read more](/details/attention-management-method.md)) `Focus` `Productivity` `attention`
- [Attention Management Over Time Management](https://hbr.org/2020/03/beyond-time-management-attention-management) - Productivity paradigm shift from managing time to managing attention and focus. Recognizes that time is fixed but attention quality varies, emphasizing directing focus to high-value activities regardless of duration. ([Read more](/details/attention-management-over-time-management.md)) `Focus` `attention` `cognitive-resources`
- [Attention Residue](https://www.sophieleroy.com/) - The cognitive phenomenon where part of your attention remains stuck on a previous task even after switching to a new one, degrading performance until you fully transition, as researched by Sophie Leroy. ## Time Management Practice

- [Pareto Principle for Time Management](https://www.paretoanalysis.tools/pareto-principle/) - Application of the 80/20 rule to productivity, stating that 80% of results come from 20% of efforts. Focus on identifying and prioritizing the critical few tasks that deliver disproportionate value. ([Read more](/details/pareto-principle-for-time-management.md)) `Prioritization` `Efficiency` `Productivity`
- [90-Minute Ultradian Cycle](https://www.bluezones.com/2020/06/how-taking-breaks-can-increase-productivity-boost-energy-levels-and-help-you-show-up-in-your-life/) - Natural biological rhythm where the brain alternates between periods of high alertness and rest every 90-120 minutes. Studies show professionals working in these cycles report 50% less mental fatigue while completing complex tasks with greater accuracy. ([Read more](/details/90-minute-ultradian-cycle.md)) `Neuroscience` `Biological Rhythms` `Breaks`
- [Accountability Partnerships](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/) - Productivity practice of partnering with someone to share goals and track progress. Regular check-ins create external pressure to follow through on commitments and maintain consistency. ([Read more](/details/accountability-partnerships.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Focus`
- [Attention Management](https://www.maurathomas.com/attention-management/) - Productivity practice focusing on consciously directing attention rather than just managing time. Emphasizes being proactive rather than reactive and maintaining control over focus in the face of constant distractions. ([Read more](/details/attention-management.md)) `Focus` `Mindfulness` `Productivity`
- [Chronotype-Based Productivity](https://www.risescience.com/blog/when-are-you-most-productive) - Productivity approach based on aligning work schedules with your natural circadian rhythm preferences. Understanding whether you're a morning lark, afternoon warrior, or night owl enables optimal task scheduling for peak performance. ([Read more](/details/chronotype-based-productivity.md)) `Energy Management` `Biological Rhythms` `Scheduling`
- [Context Switching Cost](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/) - Productivity penalty incurred when shifting between different tasks or mental modes. Research shows task switching can reduce productivity by up to 40% and requires an average of 23 minutes to fully regain focus after interruption. ([Read more](/details/context-switching-cost.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Focus`
- [Energy Audit Method](https://www.insightful.io/blog/peak-productivity-bio-prime-time) - Productivity practice involving tracking energy and focus levels throughout the day for 1-3 weeks to identify personal peak performance windows. Provides data to optimize task scheduling according to natural energy patterns. ([Read more](/details/energy-audit-method.md)) `Self Awareness` `Energy Management` `Productivity`
- [Focus Music for Productivity](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/) - Use of specially designed audio to enhance concentration and work performance. Platforms like Brain.fm and Focus@Will create functional music that coordinates brain activity to boost focus duration and intensity. ([Read more](/details/focus-music-for-productivity.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Focus`
- [Interstitial Journaling](https://nesslabs.com/interstitial-journaling) - A productivity technique combining note-taking, task management, and time tracking by writing timestamped journal entries whenever switching tasks or taking breaks, reducing context switching costs and improving mindfulness. ([Read more](/details/interstitial-journaling.md)) `Journaling` `Mindfulness` `Productivity`
- [Micro-Breaks Technique](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/) - Taking very short breaks (30 seconds to 2 minutes) throughout work sessions to reset attention and prevent fatigue. Differs from longer Pomodoro-style breaks by being briefer and more frequent. ([Read more](/details/micro-breaks-technique.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Focus`
- [Scheduled Breaks Method](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/) - Productivity practice of planning breaks at specific intervals rather than taking them randomly. Prevents decision fatigue about when to rest and ensures consistent recovery periods throughout the workday. ([Read more](/details/scheduled-breaks-method.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Focus`
- [Sequential Tasking](https://friday.app/p/multi-single-tasking) - Work methodology emphasizing completing tasks one after another in order rather than attempting parallel processing. Aligns with cognitive research showing the brain's inability to truly multitask, reducing errors and improving output quality. ([Read more](/details/sequential-tasking.md)) `Focus` `Efficiency` `Task Management`
- [Time Audit](https://www.lucidchart.com/blog/how-to-do-a-time-audit) - Systematic examination of how time is actually spent over a period, tracking activities to identify patterns, time-wasting habits, and opportunities for more intentional time allocation. ([Read more](/details/time-audit.md)) `Analysis` `Productivity` `Self Awareness`
- [Time Blocking Planner](https://www.timeblockplanner.com/) - Physical or digital planners specifically designed for time blocking methodology, with hourly grids and structured layouts. Helps visualize daily schedules, allocate time to specific tasks, and maintain focus through intentional time allocation. ([Read more](/details/time-blocking-planner.md)) `Time Blocking` `Planning` `Scheduling`
- [Timeboxing](https://clockify.me/timeboxing) - Time management technique where you allocate a fixed, maximum time period for an activity, declaring your work done when time expires regardless of completion status. ([Read more](/details/timeboxing.md)) `Time Management` `Focus` `Deadlines`
- [Two-Minute Rule](https://gettingthingsdone.com/2020/05/the-two-minute-rule-2/) - David Allen's GTD principle stating that if an action takes less than two minutes, it should be done immediately rather than added to a task list, preventing small tasks from accumulating and clogging your system. ([Read more](/details/two-minute-rule.md)) `Productivity` `Quick Tasks` `Gtd`
- [Virtual Coworking](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/) - Online platforms enabling remote workers to work alongside others via video or shared digital spaces. Creates accountability, reduces isolation, and mimics office atmosphere for distributed teams. ([Read more](/details/virtual-coworking.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Focus`

- [chronicl](https://groups.google.com/g/mechanical-sympathy/c/W31SWkqyv_w) - A graphical Python utility for visualizing historical Toggl time-tracking data, helping users explore and analyze how their time is spent over time. ([Read more](/details/chronicl.md))

- [guzzle-toggl](https://github.com/arendjantetteroo/guzzle-toggl) - A PHP library for Toggl API v8 built on the Guzzle HTTP client, allowing PHP applications to integrate with Toggl’s time-tracking features programmatically. ([Read more](/details/guzzle-toggl.md))
- [Ixudra Toggl (Laravel PHP library)](https://devforum.roblox.com/t/full-tutorial-how-to-script-on-roblox-beginners/1991385) - A Laravel-focused PHP library for Toggl API v8 that simplifies integrating Toggl time-tracking capabilities into Laravel applications. ([Read more](/details/ixudra-toggl-laravel-php-library.md))
- [JToggl](https://github.com/bbaumgartner/jtoggl/blob/master/README.md) - A Java wrapper for the Toggl time-tracking API v8, providing an object-oriented interface for integrating Toggl time tracking into Java applications. ([Read more](/details/jtoggl.md))
- [Morning Train Toggl API (PHP classes)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morning) - A set of PHP classes for interacting with the Toggl API v8, based on Guzzle 6, providing a structured way to integrate Toggl time-tracking into PHP projects. ([Read more](/details/morning-train-toggl-api-php-classes.md))
- [node-toggl-api](https://zenodo.org/records/15048694/files/RQ2_1_DiD_data.csv?download=1) - A Node.js library for the Toggl API v8 that provides a programmatic interface for integrating Toggl time tracking into JavaScript/Node applications. ([Read more](/details/node-toggl-api.md))
- [Plasmoggl](https://github.com/FedericoVaga/plasmoggl) - A KDE plasmoid that integrates Toggl Track time tracking into the KDE desktop, allowing users to start and manage Toggl timers directly from their panel. ([Read more](/details/plasmoggl.md))
- [time_tracker_cube](https://time.is/) - An Arduino-based physical time tracking cube that uses the Toggl Track API. Each side of the paper cube can be configured to start tracking a different task when that side faces upwards. ([Read more](/details/time_tracker_cube.md))
- [Toggl Track API](https://www.hotbot.com/articles/maximize-your-productivity-an-in-depth-toggl-track-user-guide/) - A RESTful JSON API for Toggl’s time tracking service, enabling developers and third‑party applications to programmatically manage users, projects, and time entries in Toggl Track. It supports ISO 8601/RFC 3339 timestamps, per-user API tokens for authentication, and rate limiting via a leaky bucket algorithm, making it suitable for building custom time tracking integrations, automations, and reporting tools. ([Read more](/details/toggl-track-api.md))
- [Toggl Track Webhooks API](https://developers.track.toggl.com/docs/) - The Toggl Track Webhooks API lets developers subscribe to workspace events and receive callbacks when time tracking or related data changes. It is used to monitor time entry updates, synchronize Toggl data with other systems in near real time, and trigger automations based on tracking activity. ([Read more](/details/toggl-track-webhooks-api.md))
- [toggl_target](https://mosab.co.uk/toggl_target/) - A Python project that uses the Toggl API to calculate how many hours you need to work to hit monthly goals, extending Toggl time tracking with goal management and planning capabilities. ([Read more](/details/toggl_target.md))
- [Toggl-API-Wrapper-Perl](https://toggl.com/track/automated-time-tracker/) - A Perl wrapper for the Toggl API that creates and authenticates sessions for API v8 or v2 depending on the URL, and implements functions for exporting time tracking reports. ([Read more](/details/toggl-api-wrapper-perl.md))
- [toggl-hammer](https://github.com/david-cako/toggl-hammer) - An efficient Python command-line interface for quickly entering time-insensitive Toggl entries, streamlining bulk or approximate time tracking from the terminal. ([Read more](/details/toggl-hammer.md))
- [Toggl-Report-Api](https://github.com/rocketbase-io/toggl-report-api) - A Java wrapper for the Toggl Reports API that simplifies accessing and processing detailed time-tracking reports from Toggl in Java applications. ([Read more](/details/toggl-report-api.md))
- [toggl-reporter](https://toggl.com/) - A Spring Boot application built with Vaadin and MongoDB that imports your Toggl TimeEntries to provide fine-grained reporting and analysis, including detailed working hours comparisons between team members. A Docker image is also available for easy deployment. ([Read more](/details/toggl-reporter.md))
- [Toggl-Swift](https://toggl.com/) - A macOS Swift 4.2 wrapper for the Toggl API v8, designed to make it easier to integrate Toggl time tracking functionality into native Mac applications. ([Read more](/details/toggl-swift.md))
- [togglex](https://github.com/diacode/togglex) - An Elixir wrapper for Toggl API v8 and the Toggl Reports API, allowing Elixir applications to integrate with Toggl’s time-tracking and reporting capabilities. ([Read more](/details/togglex.md))
- [togglore](https://github.com/toggl/toggl_api_docs) - A simple Python tool that compares tracked hours in Toggl with expected working hours over a given period, supporting auditing and workload analysis of time-tracking data. ([Read more](/details/togglore.md))
- [TogglPy](https://github.com/matthewdowney/TogglPy/pulse) - A Python wrapper for the Toggl API that allows developers to integrate Toggl time tracking features into Python applications with a simplified interface. ([Read more](/details/togglpy.md))
- [togglr](https://www.togglr.com/advisory-and-implementation-services) - An R and RStudio client for the Toggl API that lets users access and analyze their Toggl time tracking data directly from R. ([Read more](/details/togglr.md))
- [togglv8](https://github.com/kanet77/togglv8) - A Ruby wrapper for the Toggl API v8 that enables Ruby applications and scripts to interact with Toggl time tracking (e.g., managing time entries, projects, and clients). ([Read more](/details/togglv8.md))

## Pomodoro Technique

- [NatureTimer](https://www.naturetimer.com/) - Free web-based Pomodoro timer with automatic work/break cycling and health reminders every 20 minutes to prevent eye strain using the 20-20-20 rule. Track productivity streaks and boost focus with nature-themed timer interface and customizable work sessions. ([Read more](/details/naturetimer.md)) `Web Based` `Free` `Eye Health`

## practices

- [Fair Labor Standards Act (FLSA)](https://www.dol.gov/agencies/whd/flsa) - The Fair Labor Standards Act (FLSA) is a U.S. federal law that sets minimum wage, overtime pay, recordkeeping, and youth employment standards. For time tracking, it defines how working hours and overtime must be recorded and paid, making accurate, auditable time and attendance tracking systems essential for compliance. Time tracking tools used in U.S. organizations must support reliable capture of hours worked, visibility into overtime, and long-term retention of editable history to satisfy FLSA requirements and reduce risk of non-compliance and payroll disputes. ([Read more](/details/fair-labor-standards-act-flsa.md)) `Compliance` `hr` `attendance`
- [SSAE 18 (Statement on Standards for Attestation Engagements No. 18)](https://windes.com/ssae-18-compliance/) - SSAE 18 is an attestation standard used for auditing and reporting on the controls of service organizations, such as cloud-based time tracking and attendance providers. Vendors that undergo SSAE 18/SOC examinations demonstrate that they maintain appropriate controls over security, availability, processing integrity, confidentiality, and privacy. For time tracking, choosing an SSAE 18-audited provider helps ensure that employee time data is securely stored, properly backed up, and reliably available, supporting compliance, auditability, and disaster recovery requirements. ([Read more](/details/ssae-18-statement-on-standards-for-attestation-engagements-no-18.md)) `Compliance` `Enterprise` `privacy`
